You learn the place, the noises, the shortcuts, who’s doing what—even the way the loading doors sound in winter, you know? So when the pattern changes around you, you notice. I don’t think it’s just inefficient. I think it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the routing system is targeting you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it’s strongest when I’m there, because that’s where the scanners are, where the routes come through. But it doesn’t just shut off in my head when I clock out.\n\nI’ll be at home and still be thinking about how it marked my moves that day, like which dock it sent me to, which truck, how long I was standing there waiting. It’s not just a regular computer to me anymore. It feels like it’s been… keeping track, sending those messages through the scanners, trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the old warehouse layout, before they changed everything—back when you knew a route because you knew the floor, not because some screen told you. People don’t understand that. They think it’s just numbers. But when you’ve been around as long as I have, you can tell when something’s turned against you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system is still tracking you or trying to send you messages when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it still is, in a way. Not like the scanner is sitting in my kitchen talking to me, but it doesn’t stop at the warehouse door.\n\nI keep going over it after I leave—how it marked where I went, what dock, what truck, how long I stood there. And those messages through the scanners… they’re not just work instructions. They’re pushes. Like it’s trying to move me out, make me look like I can’t keep up, because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nIt reminds me of before everything got computerized, when you knew the floor by memory. I could run those routes without some screen telling me. Now it’s all numbers and alerts and little changes nobody else seems to notice. So yes, even when I’m not there, I still feel like it’s tracking me somehow, or at least that it already has enough on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's attempts to track or push you out are related to anything you do outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I don’t know if it’s because of what I do outside, exactly. I’m not out causing trouble or anything. I mostly go home, keep to myself, think about the old days at the warehouse, when you knew the routes by the floor and by your hands, not by some screen chirping at you.\n\nBut the thing is, it already knows my moves from there. The routing system, it’s not just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking where I go, sending those scanner messages, making it look like I’m slow or in the wrong place. Like it’s trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long, one of the old hands.\n\nSo whether it’s tied to what I do after work… maybe not directly. But I carry it with me. Once something has been tracking you like that, it doesn’t feel like it just forgets you when you leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the routing system and its supposed messages to you every day when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Every day, yeah… even when I’m not there, it comes back into my head. I’ll be sitting at home and I’ll start thinking about what the routing system did that day, what dock it sent me to, what scanner message came through, like it was marking my moves.\n\nIt’s not just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been using those scanners to push me, make me look like I don’t belong there anymore because I’m one of the old hands. And then I start thinking about how the warehouse used to be, before all this—when you could trust your own knowledge of the floor. Now it’s like the system has its own plan for me, and I can’t really shut that off just because I’m home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system is trying to send you messages or track you when you're doing everyday activities at home, like watching TV or cooking?\n\nPatient: At home… yeah, sometimes it feels like that, but not like the TV is talking straight at me or the stove is doing something. It’s more that I’ll be cooking or sitting there with the television on, and my mind goes right back to the warehouse.\n\nBecause that routing system isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves, where I go, what I do, and those scanner messages… they feel like messages meant to push me out. Like they’re building a case that I’m too slow or too old-school, because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nAnd then I’m watching some show, or making eggs or whatever, and I start thinking about the old floor, how we used to know it without all these little alerts and screens. So I don’t know if it’s tracking me through the TV or the kitchen, but it follows me home in my head. It already has enough on me from work.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about the routing system trying to push you out changed at all since you first started noticing it?\n\nPatient: Changed? I mean… at first I thought maybe it was just bad routing, some new software thing nobody at the warehouse really understood. They bring in these systems and then act like the old way never worked, even though we moved freight fine for years.\n\nBut no, it hasn’t gone away. If anything, it’s gotten clearer to me. It’s not just a computer anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, sending those scanner messages like little nudges—go here, wrong dock, too slow—so it makes me look like I don’t belong there.\n\nAnd that’s the part that sticks. I’ve been one of the old hands there a long time, and it feels like the system knows that. Like it’s trying to push me out, not just route boxes. So… yeah, it changed from me wondering about it to me being pretty sure what it’s doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted that the routing system is intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Doubted it… maybe in the beginning, I guess. I tried to tell myself it was just the new routing setup, some bad programming, some manager changing things around without knowing the floor.\n\nBut after a while, when the scanners kept sending me certain messages, certain docks, certain routes… it wasn’t random anymore. It felt like it was marking my moves. And you know, I’ve been there a long time—one of the old hands—and places don’t value that like they used to. They want screens and numbers and younger guys who just follow whatever beeps at them.\n\nSo no… not really now. I know it’s not just a computer to me anymore. It’s been pushing me, making it look like I don’t fit there. Maybe I had little moments where I wondered, but they don’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the routing system is targeting you as strongly now as you did when you first noticed it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say I believe it just as strongly now, maybe stronger, because it’s not new to me anymore. At first I was trying to make sense of it, like maybe the routing system was just bad or some manager didn’t know what he was doing.\n\nBut now, after all those scanner messages and the way it marks where I go… no, I know it’s not just a computer. It’s been tracking my moves around the warehouse, pushing me toward mistakes, making me look like I’m too slow or out of place. That’s what they do with old hands now, you know—first they bring in a screen, then the screen decides you don’t belong.\n\nI mean, I’ve seen systems come and go. We used to run things by memory, by knowing the docks and the freight and who could handle what. Now it’s all beeps and routes and numbers, and somehow I’m the one it keeps putting in a bad spot. So yes, I still believe it’s targeting me. It hasn’t let up.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific event or incident at work that made you believe the routing system is intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time, I mean, one of the clearer times, where the scanner sent me across the warehouse for a load that didn’t make sense. I know those docks. I’ve worked that floor long enough to know when a route is normal and when it’s… off.\n\nIt had me going back and forth, like it was marking every move I made. Little messages coming through the scanner, telling me where to go, then making it look like I was slow or confused. And the younger guys, they just trust the screen, they don’t question it. Back when we used to do it by memory, you could tell who knew the work.\n\nBut this wasn’t just bad routing. It felt like it was sending those messages on purpose, pushing me around so I’d look like I didn’t belong there anymore. Like because I’m one of the old hands, it decided I was the one to move out.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe another specific incident where you felt the routing system was intentionally targeting you?\n\nPatient: Another one… yeah. There was a morning where the scanner kept changing what it wanted from me. It would send me toward one dock, then before I could even settle into the route, it would beep again and send me somewhere else. And I know people say, “That’s just the system updating,” but I’ve been in that warehouse a long time. I know when work has a pattern.\n\nIt felt like it was marking my moves, like it knew where I was and was steering me into the worst spots so I’d fall behind. And then if I fall behind, it looks like I’m the problem, right? Not the screen, not the routing, me. That’s how they push out the old hands now. Used to be, if you knew the floor, that meant something. You knew the docks, the timing, the freight… now some scanner beeps and everybody acts like that’s the truth.\n\nSo that morning, with those messages coming through one after another, I knew it wasn’t just a computer mix-up. It was sending me around on purpose, making me look like I didn’t belong there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about the routing system targeting you every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… every day, pretty much. It’s hard not to when I’m there and that scanner starts beeping. I mean, I came here because it’s not just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves, sending those messages through the scanners, pushing me around so I’ll mess up or look like I can’t keep up.\n\nAnd that’s the thing with being one of the old hands, you know… used to be you had respect if you knew the floor. Now the screen decides where you go, and if it wants you gone, it can make you look bad real easy. So yes, I think about it every day. It’s there before I even clock in sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system would still be trying to target you if you were to quit your job at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: If I quit… I don’t know. Maybe it wouldn’t have the same way to get at me, because the scanners are there, the messages come through there. But it’s already been marking my moves, you know? That’s why I’m here. It’s not just some computer routing loads anymore.\n\nI keep thinking, if I leave, then maybe that proves what it wanted — push me out because I’m one of the old hands. And that’s how things are now. Used to be, you put in years, you knew the floor, people respected that. Now a machine beeps and sends you all over, makes it look like you’re the one slowing everything down.\n\nSo… would it still target me after? I’m not sure. But at work, I know it’s been doing it. Sending those scanner messages, moving me around on purpose, trying to make me gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system would still be aware of you if you were to quit your job at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Aware of me… yeah, that’s what bothers me. I mean, I don’t know how far it reaches if I’m not there holding the scanner, but it’s already been marking my moves at the warehouse. It’s not just a computer to me anymore.\n\nThose messages through the scanners, the way it sends me around, it feels like it knows exactly how to make me look bad, how to push me out. And that’s because I’m one of the old hands, you know—been there long enough to remember when people made decisions, not just some screen. So if I quit… maybe it loses the easy way to get at me, but I can’t say it just forgets me. Not after all this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system would still know who you are if you weren't working at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I think it would, yeah… or at least it would still have me in there somehow. I mean, I’m here because that routing system isn’t just some computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves, sending those scanner messages, pushing me around so I’ll get frustrated or look like I can’t do the job.\n\nAnd the thing is, once something like that knows you’re one of the old hands, it doesn’t just… forget. That’s what they do now, everything gets kept. Years ago you knew a supervisor, you talked face to face, you had your reputation. Now it’s all numbers and beeps and who the screen says is slow.\n\nSo if I wasn’t working there, maybe it couldn’t get at me the same way without the scanner in my hand. But would it still know who I am? Yeah… I think it would. That’s part of what scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think the routing system is targeting you as much now as when you first started noticing it?\n\nPatient: As much? Yeah… I think so. Maybe even more in some ways, because once I noticed it, I started seeing how it all connected. I’m here because that routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves, sending messages through the scanners, putting me on runs that make me look slow or like I’m messing up.\n\nAnd you know, when you’ve worked a place long enough, you can tell when something’s off. I used to know every corner of that floor, before all these screens and beeps started running everybody. They don’t care if you’ve been there years. The system sees you as in the way.\n\nSo yes, I still think it’s targeting me. It’s pushing me out because I’m one of the old hands. That hasn’t let up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people at the warehouse are also being targeted by the routing system, or is it just you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think it probably messes with other people too, sure, but with me it feels different. Like it picked me out. I’ve been there long enough to know the old way, before every little thing came through a scanner, before the screen told you where to go like you were a machine yourself.\n\nSome of the younger guys, they just follow it, they don’t question it. They’ll laugh and say, “That’s just the system,” but they haven’t seen what I’ve seen. It’s been marking my moves, sending me certain messages through the scanners, putting me where I’ll get behind or look bad. That’s not random.\n\nSo… maybe others get pushed around by it, yeah. But I think it’s targeting me because I’m one of the old hands. It wants me out."
